## Preflight and external parity

## Dependency checkpoints

1. Common team/access authority: additive records, current-session/user checks, explicit capabilities and immutable responsible-team binding; synthetic allowed/denied proof. No guessed legacy mapping or Supervisor activation.
2. Private transfer and minimum recovery: exact accepted Finish/content receipts, recovery cutoffs/export, review and package/Send; retain existing photo/action/session owners.
3. Phase 6 workflow: bounded workspace, templates, ACK/follow-ups, bulk review and cross-run selection; same Phase 5 transfer/delivery/cleanup engine.
4. Shared controls, optional alerts/recovery email: complete permission matrix, scoped dispatch and actual configured TEST sender/device evidence.
5. Combine compatible Phase 5/6 phone/laptop/provider checks, retaining separate phase completion evidence; final exact-head automation/live parity and explicit Level 3 merge approval.

Independent approved work may proceed without letter-level approval. A setup/evidence failure stops only work depending on it.

## Checkpoint 2 foundation — fixed photo recovery cutoffs, 2026-10-07

Level 3 continues under the approved combined scope; runtime merge approval remains PENDING. Source rollback for this increment is `4adb153534e8d1719a33b09ffdb3d1db9ccc9caa`. The CLI-created `20261007172131_phase_5_exact_photo_recovery.sql` adds private fixed grant/member records, immutable photo identity, one shared capability owner, service-only snapshot/completion functions and self-only recovery/accepted-Finish predicates. A reviewed access revision and explicit removed office teams are checked against current trusted Auth and work scope before capture; Contractor capture includes only their own registered photo identities. Owner entitlement grants no customer-work scope. All tables use RLS and explicit revokes; service callers can read, but only the trusted functions write. Fixed-order table locks with a five-second cutoff timeout serialize evidence/access changes. These conservative locks are a contention boundary to exercise before live activation; no production-volume throughput claim is made.

The snapshot fixes photo/WO/run/capturer/capture-time/Finish/assignment/requirement and digest identity, and separately pins whether a matching Finish was already accepted. PENDING grants expose nothing. READY completion and identical action retries use the materialized list, never a new query of old WOs. Changed action inputs fail; changed evidence bindings, foreign scopes, another UUID sharing an email, revoked/expired sessions and security-locked identities fail closed. Gallery eligibility requires a current session created within 30 minutes; pre-cutoff Finish eligibility remains bound to the original capturing identity and its valid session. Neither predicate alone authorizes byte upload or read.

Embedded PostgreSQL 0.3.14 applied all 26 migrations and passed all three focused Phase 5 gates. The rollback-only gate distinguishes accepted Finish from registered metadata and a Finish accepted after cutoff; proves all three Supervisor scopes, PENDING denial, fixed retries, post-cutoff photo exclusion, immutable binding and identity/session boundaries. No real photo bytes or client delivery is fabricated. Native multi-connection and exact-head CI evidence is pending publication of this increment.

**Delivered as source-only implementations:**
- `supabase/migrations/20261010130000_phase_5_delivery_fencing.sql`: separate additive DB candidate after all previous 5D schemas. A service-role-only, single-package `SKIP LOCKED` claim fences by worker UUID, increasing generation and short lease; default delivery runtime remains OFF. No browser/ordinary Auth role may call worker functions or read private remote evidence. An expired attempt is quarantined `UNCERTAIN`, not requeued with a new remote ID. The immutable release manifest now includes each selected private photo's exact receipt size in addition to SHA-256.
- Remote file identity journal enforces **generated/persisted** Drive IDs for one package folder, each exact selected photo UUID and one submission JSON; exact parent is the company's frozen destination root or preallocated package folder. Identical reservation replays; changed ID/parent and unrelated photo fail closed. Fresh fenced worker status is mandatory before reservations or confirmation. Service confirmation requires exactly matching provider-observed ID/parent/MIME/SHA-256/size; missing/inaccessible Google metadata is unresolved, not success. `DELIVERED` requires complete verified folder+selected files+manifest and atomically commits an immutable final receipt. This is a trusted-server receipt gate, not evidence of a real Google request until the worker actually authenticates and calls `files.get`.
- `supabase/functions/client-delivery/drive-client.mjs`: fixed Google API origin and non-redirecting OAuth-bearing requests; explicit Drive API v3 `about.get` identity proof, `files.get` destination folder capability and shared Drive ID proof, bounded `files.generateIds`, preallocated folder create, strict resumable-session-origin and range handling, and exact independent final metadata verification. A 404/410, 409, missing hash, network uncertainty, or inaccessible folder cannot silently create another file. No token is embedded in GitHub source.
- `supabase/functions/client-delivery/worker-core.mjs`: a pure **preflight/preallocation coordinator**, not a deployed worker. Checks the frozen DB claim, original configured Workspace account, company destination and photo list; then generates all Drive IDs and saves each under the fenced lease *before* any provider create/upload. If an identity check, lease or reservation fails, there is no remote create or blind retry.
- Automated controlled tests include role-denied claim, competing claim, generation/worker mismatch, foreign remote-ID replacement, checksum/parent mismatch, premature finalization, one complete synthetic delivery receipt, duplicate finalization refusal and unaffected ordinary photo review. Mocked Drive tests cover OAuth account, folder capabilities/shared Drive context, GET SHA/size, generated IDs, valid/invalid upload sessions, offsets/chunks, ambiguous network outcomes and all-ID-first preallocation. Authorized `admin_correction_state` reads the selected scoped WO's accepted FIELD_COMPLETE run, true original release goals, server-classified INTERNAL_CORRECTION/CLIENT_RETURN, original current run/updated_at, client company and current assignment; blocks unresolved protected old photos and uncertain/failed/in-flight/queued delivery. `admin_save_correction_draft` stores exactly one CAS-revisioned draft per (WO UUID, origin run UUID), with new capture requirements, explicit UUID capture-to-release item mapping, nonrelaxing release snapshot, reference photo UUIDs, reason, new contractor/due date/instructions and author. Stale draft edits, foreign photo references, unauthorized contractor/team, incomplete Finish and stale WO revision fail closed. `admin_preview_correction_draft` saves a checked SHA-256 manifest-revision/actor receipt, without dispatch; `admin_dispatch_correction_draft` requires the exact saved revision and same current actor's checked preview, then calls the **existing Phase 6B `private.admin_create_followup`** under its original WO lock and protected new-run transaction. **Approved ownership:** One server-controlled operational issue source, optional per-user per-type channels, and a private worker only. No new SMS/email platform, native Admin app, Android camera/field-action worker, job-completion or storage cleanup owner. Nine stable alert types have independent `Dashboard` default **On**, `Phone`/**Computer` default **Off**; changing alert preference never hides the underlying protected job problem or marks work Complete. Server issues derive only persisted current work/assignment/confirmed ACK, accepted Finish and private receipt, configured organization due-date timezone and confirmed delivery/package history. `ACCEPT_REMINDER` is separately selectable with 24h default and exact current instance, no human Accept inferred. `OVERDUE` needs explicitly configured IANA organization timezone (including America/Chicago supported), rather than a guessed browser timezone; quiet hours are optional per-user/per-type, and respect local IANA time. `UPLOAD_PROBLEM` **deliberately has no automatic producer** until an authoritative server progress heartbeat exists; current private transfer state has WAITING/RECEIVED only. `APP_OR_SYNC_PROBLEM` also remains unproduced without exact accepted fault report. Unknown offline device conditions are not fabricated as server issues.

**Additive source-only migrations, no hosted activation:**
- `20261011222000_phase_6e_alerts_foundation.sql`: private RLS/grants for per-user alert settings, org timezones, deduplicated current issue versions, read/snooze receipts, private Web Push endpoint/key subscriptions, service outbox and immutable replay journal; security-invoker public RPCs, trusted private owners.
- `20261011223000_phase_6e_alert_facts.sql`: server-derived bounded 25/50 alert list and limited 50-most-recent-scoped-job evaluation. `has_more_unverified` acknowledges when the server has more than the bounded 50 WO context; no bogus claim of a complete global feed. Acknowledge hides informational delivery only, does not resolve ongoing field issue.
- `20261011224000_phase_6e_alert_actions_push_enrollment.sql`: idempotent expected-revision per-user preferences, ACK/snooze, explicit device UUID + class + exact account/org enrollment, disable/revoke and strict public-HTTPS provider endpoint/key validation. An unsupported oversized PostgreSQL regex range caused an intermediate SQL failure, and a subsequent file-edit inadvertently duplicated portions of this migration; it was fully restored from the original single-source migration and re-patched with explicit bounded length and alphabet validation. Final exact-head native PostgreSQL tests PASS and each definition occurs once.
- `20261011225000_phase_6e_push_runtime_gate.sql`: real Org-level VAPID **public** key and verified worker status are required; default push_ready false. Private VAPID signing key is backend environment-only; not in database/UI/repo.
- `20261011226000_phase_6e_push_dispatch_owner.sql`: service-role-only bounded issue scanner, unique org/user/device/issue-version queued receipts, original work/team/supervisor status reevaluation and send-time revalidation of unchanged job/ACK/quiet/snooze/channel/enrollment; exact durable lease/generation checks and provider response journal (PUSH_ACCEPTED_BY_SERVICE vs FAILED/EXPIRED/CANCELLED). 404/410 expires device, 429/5xx backs off. No provider success is presented as OS display or human read, and a genuinely unknown HTTP/network outcome holds for reconciliation rather than blind retry. An earlier unavailable `auth.role()` function was corrected to use existing project's service-role JWT check. Final native tests include a **disposable explicit fake enrollment**, synthetic accepted-by-provider 202 receipt, idempotent scope and unchanged human ACK, then transaction ROLLBACK.

**Small existing dashboard UI:** `dashboard/notifications.js` and `index.html` add a collapsed Alert Center with nine per-type channel switches, optional quiet start/end and timezone, opt-in 24h Accept reminder delay, current bounded issues, Acknowledge/Snooze and exact guarded work-order opening. It reuses the signed-in Admin/session and `JobWorkspace.choose`, clearing visible alerts on account switch/signout. `dashboard/push-device.js` requests browser permission **only on explicit Enable** for eligible Chrome/Edge desktop or Android Chrome, uses only local non-secret device UUID/revision/pending-revoke marker, and attempts same-actor server revoke on signout; on offline signout it unsubscribes locally and records a pending revoke for same-account authenticated reconciliation. Unsupported browsers remain unsupported pending reality tests. `dashboard/push-sw.js` is push/click only with generic lock-screen text, opaque alert UUID and relative hosted-dashboard link, with no offline/cache/photo/provider credentials or background work owner. The GitHub Pages workflow's dashboard directory path was checked; no root slash assumed. The service worker is NOT installed by opening the settings page.

**Private Push adapter:** `supabase/functions/admin-web-push/handler.mjs` and `index.ts` are disabled by default, require trusted fixed worker secret and environment-pinned VAPID keys/contact, source-only Deno npm Web Push adapter, bounded at 500 work orders and 5 provider sends per invocation. FCM/Mozilla/Windows/Apple public provider allowlisting, opaque alert-only payload, no CORS or public status, exact service ledger preflight and no blind requeue on unknown remote outcome. No scheduler/cron, private VAPID keys, active provider credentials or endpoint enrollment was set on any real deployment. `tests/functions/phase_6_admin_push.test.mjs` exercises service-only authorization, SSRF/non-HTTPS rejection, generic payload, 410, unknown outcome and bounded pagination without any network/provider calls.

## Phase 6F — exact photo recovery on Supervisor scope loss (2026-10-11)

**Classification / authority:** In the unchanged approved Phase 5/6 plan and Supervisor amendment, Level 3, scope key `dashboard-workflow-enhancement-plan`, single draft PR #40 branch `feat/phase-5-6-dashboard-workflow`. Baseline/rollback for this focused source batch: `1eb876713bc651b2cdaaff18246f1e42de4d6eb3`; known-good governed main `62244e2c4c11831d4e8104821f851f19124b2616`. Affected owners: existing Product Owner tier/scope RPCs and existing exact recovery snapshot helper; no new photo, Storage, access or cleanup owner. Rules: AGENTS, GOVERNANCE, PROJECT_PROFILE, RULE_INDEX, CHANGE_CONTROL, TESTING, INTEGRATION, phase staging, approved Phase 5/6 plans and shared-access/recovery amendment.

**Root gap:** Draft `owner_change_supervisor_tier` permitted Tier 3 → Tier 1/2 and `owner_set_supervisor_team_scope` permitted revoking a granted team with no fixed historical photo cutoff. The approved amendment requires preserving already-visible photo UUIDs before loss of work scope. The existing Phase 5 `freeze_photo_recovery_scope`/READY member owner already provides the required bounded immutable snapshot; bypassing it was inconsistent with section 6 of the approved amendment. No evidence suggests these draft-only RPCs have been deployed.

**Implemented source-only:** Commits `c7ede747c27df8244d9271a66ca7c71461f0f8e6` and `bfa04e5959ae9e8409c6e9d470ec2b5e2e660cca` amend the already-undelivered Phase 6F tier/scope migrations. Under the existing lifecycle lock, a live Tier 3 downgrade first freezes only active organization teams not retained by the explicit new scoped tier; a live exact-team scope removal first freezes that team. Both invoke the existing private trusted immutable recovery helper, complete the snapshot to READY before removing permission, and fail the whole transaction if cutoff fails. No cutoff is invented for an inactive/not-yet-enabled account, whose disablement/no-work gate already applies; Tier 1 ↔ Tier 2 adjustments without loss of WORK-scope do not manufacture unnecessary photo grants. Grants, photos, work/run identity, client delivery and cleanup remain unchanged. Source migration edits are restricted to undelivered draft source; hosted Team migration history was not touched.

**Tests:** Existing PostgreSQL authority fixture expanded in commits `e1be083a711faa5eb8ad705108b71057547f9772` and `358982a171cf0f9397addc368a3416dea6665ad7` to require READY fixed cutoff on team scope removal, and on organization Tier 3 → scoped Tier 2 downgrade; retained/removed current-work boundaries and scoped restoration are checked. The separate established Phase 5 recovery fixture already exercises immutable real photo IDs (accepted and unaccepted Finish), cross-team isolation, cutoff retry and future-photo exclusion. These new 6F checks use synthetic zero-photo work scopes and therefore do **not**, alone, prove a real-photo Supervisor revocation outcome; that remains in the combined real evidence gate. ## Phase 6F — existing verified Supervisor enrollment and grant lifecycle (2026-10-12)

**Authority/classification:** Approved whole Phase 5/6 plan, including Phase 6F shared access and recovery amendment, Level 3, scope key `dashboard-workflow-enhancement-plan`, existing single draft/unmerged PR #40 on `feat/phase-5-6-dashboard-workflow`. Focused starting/rollback head `0eab062b3cdacf62a5fb1377b4d08adfece3d295`, with governed main `62244e2c4c11831d4e8104821f851f19124b2616`. Mandatory AGENTS, GOVERNANCE, PROJECT_PROFILE, RULE_INDEX, CHANGE_CONTROL, TESTING, INTEGRATION, phase staging and both approved Phase 5/6 parent plans and access/recovery amendment were reread on the head. Production release / Level 3 merge still requires explicit separate operator approval.

**Implementation / owner:** Additive undelivered migration `supabase/migrations/20261012102100_phase_6f_owner_existing_supervisor_enrollment.sql` holds immutable private `supervisor_enrollment_actions` and `supervisor_grant_state_actions`, with SQL RLS, no exposed direct client table writes and only narrow Owner-authorized RPCs. `owner_enroll_existing_supervisor` admits only the exact already-verified, already-org-bound, inactive `SUPERVISOR` Auth/work-role UUID; it validates existing ACTIVE organization team UUIDs (Tier 1/2 require ≥1, Tier 3 uses org grant), exact access revision, separately verified recent Owner session and an active reserved Supervisor seat. It consumes that reservation exactly once without activating `account_work_access`, changing Auth identity, changing `supervisor_work_ready` or authorizing any customer-photo read. Repeated requests with the same immutable action return the historical enrollment result, never re-enable a subsequently revoked grant. For the dashboard, `owner_enroll_supervisor_with_seat` composes existing owner seat reservation and exact enrollment in ONE database transaction, rolling back both if either check fails; an ambiguous browser response is retried with the same action ID, not a new speculative enrollment.

`owner_set_supervisor_grant_state` uses an independent immutable grant action with expected grant AND work-access revisions, actor/reason/environment and current Owner authentication. It requires inactive WORK before either deactivating or reactivating the grant; active Supervisor work must first be disabled by the existing **reviewed** Phase 5 account lifecycle, which stores the immutable historical photo cutoff and exact team-manager continuity. Grant removal preserves historical team scope, user UUID and photo recovery and does not delete/bypass Auth or reopen work. Grant restoration similarly leaves work OFF; full work restore separately needs the existing reviewed authorization and capacity/identity checks. No separate trust/photo-recovery owner or new direct table permission was introduced.

**Dashboard:** The pre-existing shared Owner console now offers a bounded 'Enroll a verified existing Supervisor' form populated only from server-sourced `identity_ready=true`, inactive Supervisor accounts with no prior grant and eligible scoped teams. Tier 1/2 require deliberate team checkbox selection; Tier 3 uses organization scope. One confirmation/audit reason precedes the atomic request. The grant list offers 'Remove grant' only after work has been disabled, or 'Re-enable grant' without restoring work. A removed grant blocks the unrelated 'Review restore' button until grant authority returns; reviewed disable/restore remains authoritative. In-memory pending enrollment action survives a transport failure for **same-action retry and server read reconciliation**; it is not written to browser storage and clears on signout. The current console remains pinned to TEST and the existing Admin-workspace Owner identity; it is not a new Owner-only authentication path.

**Focused native regressions:** Rollback-only `tests/supabase/phase_5_access_gate.sql` covers ordinary Admin denial, other-org team rejection, stale access/grant revisions, unconfirmed Auth identity denial, consumed seat replay/double-spend prevention, duplicate action payload denial, no seat orphan on atomic failed composition, Tier 3 organization scope, persistence of inactive work and unchanged team identity after removal/re-enable, and refusal to revoke an active Supervisor account before reviewed disablement. The existing Phase 5 recovery gate independently owns real-photo membership/cutoff tests; synthetic grant fixtures are not real-photo proof.

**Disposable browser regressions:** `tests/dashboard/browser/workspace.cjs` blocks live backend traffic and checks identity-ready filtering, no-team form denial, exact team/tier/verified UUID enrollment, duplicate candidate removal after commit, grant remove/re-enable without activating work, denial of work restoration while grant is disabled, and a simulated ambiguous enrollment response followed by **same-action UUID retry**. ## Phase 6F — safely bounded Contractor roster placement/idle transfer (2026-10-12)

**Governed line:** Existing whole Phase 5/6 Level 3 `dashboard-workflow-enhancement-plan`, PR #40 `feat/phase-5-6-dashboard-workflow`, draft/unmerged. Base and focused rollback point: `9811d1376331d1001fee057157046409b8abe43b`; governed main `62244e2c4c11831d4e8104821f851f19124b2616`. Governing AGENTS/GOVERNANCE/PROJECT_PROFILE/RULE_INDEX, CHANGE_CONTROL, TESTING, INTEGRATION, phase-staging doctrine, approved entire Phase 5/6 plans and Supervisor shared-access/recovery amendment were read and remain unchanged. External systems: only GitHub source/CI; **no** Team hosted migration, provider, Auth, permissions, production accounts, phone, real photos/Drive, live notification, website deploy or separate original FPP application/backend/data touched.

**Approved dependency and implementation:** Additive source-only `20261012113000_phase_6f_scoped_contractor_roster.sql` owns a narrow `manage_idle_contractor_roster` server RPC and append-only private `contractor_roster_actions` with immutable action UUID, exact user/org, old/new team, actor, reason, environment, expected/revision and operation kind. Tier 2/3 may place a genuinely unassigned currently verified active Contractor **only** within their existing supervised team, subject to existing available Contractor seat and pending-invitation occupancy. Only Tier 3 across both teams or verified Product Owner (configured organization) may transfer an already placed, idle Contractor. Tier 1, ordinary Admin, foreign team/organization, inactive/invalid account, same-team request, stale revision, reused action with changed manifest and exhausted seat fail closed. The action reruns the common exact server assignability predicate after placement; it never grants client photo access from an office control.

**Separate handoff/protected behavior:** Under the existing lifecycle serializing lock, the new operation checks current WO/pending assignee and latest active assignment against all non-FIELD_COMPLETE/non-CANCELLED work under a shared lock before changing a roster UUID. If there is any active/pending assignment it explicitly refuses the roster operation; future separate approved WO handoff with consent and historical photo/Finish/run binding is necessary. Ordinary roster movement does **not** rewrite WO responsible-team UUIDs, field runs, assignment instance, photos, packages, or delivery destinations. The original Contractor's own photo evidence still follows the Phase 5 identity and evidence grant owners; disabling work remains the only reviewed path for ordinary work cutoff. The new append-only roster ledger has UPDATE/DELETE denial even for a trusted accidental writer; authenticated/anonymous have no direct table grants.

**Existing shared Owner dashboard:** The existing Product Owner section now shows eligible scoped Contractor rows, current team and proposed destination, with deliberate team selection and audit reason. Unassigned placement and idle transfer buttons never imply WO transfer. The browser keeps only one in-memory immutable action for an ambiguous network/5xx outcome and reconciles current server roster before clearing it. An exact 4xx transaction rejection is definitive, so it clears the pending action; uncertain 5xx/transport retains the same ID and forbids mutation of the original payload before server reconciliation. A signed-out Owner gets no cross-session roster data.

**Focused verification:** Native rollback-only `phase_5_access_gate.sql` added Tier 1 denial, Tier 2 own-team placement and foreign-team refusal, Tier 2 attempted transfer denial, Tier 3 idle transfer, stale revisions, replay identical/changed payload, cross-org team denial, active-work transfer refusal and no WO responsible-team rewrite, as well as immutable ledger UPDATE/DELETE and post-placement server assignability. Disposable `workspace.cjs` gained an isolated fake owner roster with two teams, verified initial placement, denied busy move (409), and separately ambiguous 503 followed by same-action UUID retry. Several intermediate fixture-only failures were resolved at source: synthetic supervisor previously promoted in tier tests, specific West team and checkbox selectors once the second team was introduced, stale UI wait matching an option before commit, and the mock route formerly recognizing only `owner_*` (the new RPC is `manage_idle_contractor_roster`). A PostgreSQL quote-delimiter typo in the appended immutable audit trigger caused an intermediate failed migration run and was corrected with named `$roster_audit$` delimiters. None of those intermediate failures is accepted PASS evidence. Latest runtime test head `06b453171c9730bc70de742f3fb930ef077c2215`, exact CI still running at documentation write; read GitHub run conclusions before recording full PASS.

The approved adjacent staff work still needs Tier 2/3 secure Admin invitations/new team staffing via the existing Auth/invite owner, controlled active WO/team handoff with assignment exceptions and contractor consent, the non-Admin Supervisor work login, standalone owner/recovery-only access, exact photo export/email sender proof and real two-device/provider state. ## Phase 6F — Supervisor assignment mutation and in-progress consent guard (2026-10-12)

**Runtime through `9dea269f37aeac9a20045f267f76f74fe7c0bcfd`.** The selected-job Supervisor assignment preview is now an actual Tier 1–3 team-scoped assignment action without granting Supervisors the legacy Admin writer. Additive migration `20261012193000_phase_6f_supervisor_assignment_action.sql` adds an idempotent exact-action transaction. It rechecks live Supervisor identity/tier, exact team scope fingerprint, locked work-order revision, selected team, current candidate membership/account eligibility and active field state. For `ASSIGNED` work it changes the Contractor immediately and clears download/acceptance receipt state so the new Contractor must explicitly Accept. For `IN_PROGRESS` work it only records the pending assignee; the current Contractor remains assigned until the existing reassignment-consent owner accepts. Completed/cancelled work is rejected. The dashboard retains an unresolved action UUID after an ambiguous response and refuses changed-input retries.

Migration `20261012194000_phase_6f_reassignment_consent_guard.sql` keeps the existing `respond_reassignment` owner rather than creating a second consent engine, but strengthens acceptance by locking the work row and rechecking that the proposed Contractor is still an active, verified, unsuspended member of the work order's current responsible team through `private.is_assignable_contractor`. Rejection still only clears the pending request. This closes the time gap between Supervisor request and Contractor consent without moving old assignee authority or protected evidence early.

**Verification in progress on exact head `9dea269f37aeac9a20045f267f76f74fe7c0bcfd`.** The preceding head `6d503262b9133214bb21b7dae2cd175c7f0e57a0` passed database, dashboard/browser, Android/build and governance after browser fixtures were updated to exercise ambiguous assignment retry, changed-request refusal, server-confirmed revision propagation and explicit post-assignment Accept-not-confirmed state. Never embed release/CI write tokens or privileged Supabase credentials in the app. Separate internal and eventual production package/signing channels; the current candidate is versionCode **20**, while the evidence-preserving recovery APK is code **21**, so any future update candidate version must avoid misleading downgrade/collision (plan deliberate next version codes and paired recovery compatibility before build).

**Proposed installation owner.** Download only after direct user action to a bounded private cache location, verify the complete downloaded APK hash/package/version/signature before installation, then use the Android-supported platform installer and required install-source permission flow with explicit device confirmation. Check platform support and the exact API 26+/target-SDK behavior in native emulator/device. Do not use shell/ADB auto-installs, untrusted third-party updaters or data-clearing/uninstall commands. The very first updater-enabled version necessarily requires **one more manually installed, identically signed, verified APK**; the previously installed Phase 5 APK cannot acquire a UI button retroactively.

**Failure, safety, recovery, rollback.** No new Room schema or overwrite of existing cached WO/run, actions, queue records, protected originals, prepared derivatives, approved package/destination/receipts. A failed HTTPS request, invalid manifest, digest/signer/package mismatch, stale version, storage shortage, installer permission denial, cancelled install or OS rejected update leaves the currently installed binary and all app data untouched, shows actionable status, and allows retry later; discard only the attempted transient APK. Never switch from internal to production package/signing. A recovery APK must be explicitly paired to the same deployed Room schema and built/retained before field deployment; an older app downgrade after Room v5 is unsafe. Require no active protected-work conflict and preserve pending local evidence across process death and post-install launch. No dependency on a client Drive destination or FPP backend.

**Scope and verification matrix (proposed).** Source owners: Android update-check/download/install helper and minimal MainActivity utility UI; Android manifest/provider/installer permissions only if proven necessary; protected GitHub release/CI publishing identity workflow plus manifest/sha verification, with no broad Auth/photo/sync refactoring. Unit tests: absent/malformed manifest, wrong channel/host/redirect/package/signer/digest/version/size, duplicate download, offline, low storage, user cancellation, installer no-source-permission, old/recovery version handling, and no access to local PhotoOwner/Room/worker owners. Native CI: stable APK signer, incremental versionCodes/Room schema/retained app package, publishable release asset digest and no private keys. One real device gate: existing internal candidate **in-place** update through the new button, validated on-phone Android installer, cached WO and protected photos still available, offline work/queue retained, denied-permission/cancel leaves state intact and restart works. No repeated already accepted Phase 5 refresh smoke merely for reassurance. After a proven owner-authorized internal rollout, document the release procedure and keep rollback artifact. This design cannot be marked PASS by mocked installer tests or a successful GitHub Actions build alone.

Proceed only after one explicit **material phase-order/scope amendment** either (A) moves this complete updater design into the approved current Phase 5/6 work with a documented compatibility and release-trust decision, or (B) completes/approves the whole Phase 8 parent plan before implementing Phase 8D in dependency order. ## Admin session transport-timeout root cause and protected restoration repair (2026-10-13)

**Observed user-facing failure; link itself unchanged.** Owner reports the existing temporary GitHub Pages `/field-work-hub/phase56-review/` page opened repeatedly but first displayed "Your dashboard session expired", then, after recopying the link and signing in again, **"signal timed out"**. The original assistant-supplied URL unnecessarily included `?utm_source=chatgpt.com`; the canonical clean **same** preview path is `https://timbone72-cc.github.io/field-work-hub/phase56-review/`. Owner screenshots demonstrate the URL loaded the real Admin UI; there is **no evidence the query parameter caused the backend/auth error**. Independent read-only Team Supabase `get_project` reports project `vyocaujuwrivoqynvitm` `ACTIVE_HEALTHY`; last-two-hour anonymized gateway counts include **12 HTTP 200 /auth/v1/token**, **10 HTTP 200 /auth/v1/user**, and **1 HTTP 400 /auth/v1/token** at query time. These aggregated outcomes are not attributable to an exact attempt or credential, and do not prove every Chrome request completed or the password was valid/invalid for a specific submission. Static CDN could not be independently reached from this assistant's isolated web/container; last owner-accepted live preview verification remains prior PASS, not a fresh remote check.

**Identified source bug and Level 3 conservative session classification.** In the existing `dashboard/session.js` owner, `restoreAdminSession()` unconditionally called `clearAdminSession()` for *any* exception (including `AbortSignal.timeout(30000)` during signed data fetch), and unconditionally attempted refresh-token rotation after *any* first `fetchCurrentUser` error even if it was a network timeout rather than an expired JWT. The existing `dashboard/app.js` login flow deferred storing the verified tab session until after all initial work-list reads completed: Auth plus valid identity could succeed yet a later list timeout discarded active UI state, demanded another password submission, and never persisted that verified session. Both cases explain a plausible unnecessary repeat sign-in; neither claim a proven exact Chrome packet-level root cause. Fix remains entirely in the existing session/office identity owners: on specifically recognized transient TimeoutError/AbortError/TypeError/408/429/5xx, preserve opaque tab credentials for future authorized retry **while clearing all visible work/user state and requiring every new read to pass the server**. Genuine 400/401/403/unauthorized failures still clear session; only an explicit HTTP 401 can trigger the existing initial user-fetch refresh fallback. During password login, persist a completed *server-verified exact ADMIN/SUPERVISOR identity* if only the subsequent first work-list request times out. Never retain a session for a denied or unverified identity, expose an offline work screen, invent RLS authority, retry an uncertain mutation or change production Auth settings. The earlier corrected plain-language timeout display remains, independent of the session preservation.

**Ownership, source and verified automatic evidence.** Single scope `dashboard-workflow-enhancement-plan`, parent Phase 5/6 authorization unchanged, authoritative draft/unmerged PR #40 `feat/phase-5-6-dashboard-workflow`. Conservatively classified **Level 3** because session/refresh-token handling is an authorization-sensitive surface; explicit Level 3 operator **premerge approval remains pending**. ## Backend login timeout: pool starvation and Auth-table deadlock evidence / source repair — 2026-10-13

**Updated diagnosis, preserving earlier evidence.** Read-only bounded Team logs now establish a stronger timeout lead than the prior JWT-only hypothesis. PostgREST event_message (a field not included in the earlier gateway-only inspection) contains **81 PGRST003 entries**, first **2026-10-13 15:00:03.631590 UTC** (10:00:03 CDT), last **17:44:54.719662 UTC** at capture. Fixed-message categorization identifies connection-pool exhaustion. [Official PostgREST documentation](https://docs.postgrest.org/en/v16/references/errors.html) defines PGRST003 as HTTP 504 while waiting for a database-pool connection. This is a concrete backend timeout mechanism, independent of password-token acceptance. Gateway PGRST303 remains a separate observed fresh-token rejection; its exact reason and installed PostgREST version are still unverified. Do not treat ACTIVE_HEALTHY as proof the Data API can acquire a connection.

Privacy-safe PostgreSQL error projections show SQLSTATE **40P01** at **14:37:05 UTC**, and repeatedly at **15:02:32, 15:02:42, 15:03:20 and 15:05:21 UTC**, coinciding with earlier successful password Auth/user checks and failing subsequent work access. Parsed function context is **private.lock_legacy_work_authority -> private.guard_legacy_work_order -> private.admin_update_work_order_v4**. The error detail's relation OIDs independently resolve to **auth.sessions (16716)** and **auth.users (16499)**; modes are **ShareLock / RowExclusiveLock**. This establishes actual table-lock inversion, not merely a momentary sampled row wait. Earlier brief pg_stat_activity samples correctly did not prove a persistent blocker; the new historical error detail supplies the missing deadlock evidence.

The deployed helper was read in full and matches the source: locks auth.users before auth.sessions in SHARE mode, behind the existing private recovery-ledger lock. Its prosrc MD5 is **d8dc334409f900382a820a228602ffe0**, SECURITY DEFINER, empty search_path, lock_timeout 5s, no anon/authenticated EXECUTE and service_role EXECUTE. Git traces this helper to **7149dc3** (October 7 legacy-work cutover). It predates the successfully used October 13 preview; the failure is concurrency-dependent, so an unchanged frontend can work and later fail. Auth-pair reversal is a proven latent backend defect; its contribution to pool starvation is plausible but not yet a complete causal proof. PGRST003, JWT rejection and deadlock must not be collapsed into one conclusively proven provider bug.

No raw query text, JWT/session/user IDs, credentials, emails, request bodies or photo URLs were returned. Only fixed error categories, technical process/relation/function identifiers, safe durations and cumulative aggregate statistics were selected. pg_stat_statements figures are cumulative, not measurements of this individual login. No connection was cancelled, statistics reset, restart performed, account changed or live SQL applied.

**Scope / approved authority / rollback before candidate publication.** Same `dashboard-workflow-enhancement-plan`, Level **3**, sole draft/unmerged `feat/phase-5-6-dashboard-workflow` / PR #40. Runtime source rollback **89a3aa11f20e6756be8c20a41be5783817fb2229**; main **62244e2c4c11831d4e8104821f851f19124b2616**; accepted dashboard diagnostic runtime d2f5203 stays intact. The operator's "continue" authorizes continued source diagnosis/repair, not a live database deployment or Level 3 merge.

**Candidate boundary.** The pinned official Supabase CLI **2.101.0** created actual **20261013175624_phase_5_auth_session_lock_order.sql**. It changes only the existing helper's Auth-table ordering to **sessions before users**, matching the competing sessions->users write order observed in the deadlock. All lock modes/tables, recovery-ledger ordering, timeout, security-definer/empty-path configuration, privileges and downstream current-session/team/role predicates stay intact. ### Remaining login regression — PostgREST 14.5 internal conflict retry storm (2026-10-13)

**Scope and disposition.** Continued operator-authorized read-only backend diagnosis after the narrowly approved lock-order application. No further DDL, connection cancellation/termination, restart, settings, grants, secrets, Auth attempts, passwords, Pages publication or FPP mutation. This entry supersedes the earlier ranking of the observed Auth-table inversion as the strongest overall explanation of continuing login timeouts. That inversion is a real, reproduced and repaired defect; the remaining API unavailability now has stronger independent evidence.

**Provider version and observed mechanism.** Fresh pg_stat_activity identifies the active API application as **PostgREST 14.5**, user **authenticator**. Ten live connections exactly match ten process IDs repeatedly raising **40001** in existing Postgres logs. Seven execute `admin_correction_state`, reaching `private.admin_correction_basis` and its fixed **Current completed client work required** rejection; three execute `admin_update_work_order_v4`, reaching `private.admin_set_photo_requirements_phase4_engine` and fixed **Requirements changed. Reload and retry.** rejection. These are deliberate business-precondition/revision conflicts, not recovered evidence of true database serialization failures. No JWT, request SQL arguments, account/customer identity or raw data were exported.

In **18:56:49–19:02:00 UTC**, observed logs contain **31,101 SQLSTATE 40001 errors** from those ten processes: 29,686 correction eligibility, 1,415 requirements revision conflicts. Only eleven gateway events occur in that window, including OPTIONS and Auth. The repeated internal errors are therefore not explained by equivalent new incoming browser requests. Cumulative request-context setup counters also rose from 461,843,283 to 461,896,659 during this diagnosis; these are not treated as per-login counts. Individual activity query ages are only milliseconds because retries create new transactions continuously; short current query/transaction ages do not establish a healthy pool. The observed ten worker connections are distinct from the separate idle API listener. PostgreSQL max_connections is 60 and reserve 3; observed project activity does not show global connection-limit saturation. Configured PostgREST pool capacity is not exposed by the inspected database settings, so the exact internal limit is not asserted from defaults.

**Evidence-backed timeline (2026-10-13 UTC / Central daylight time).**

- The three requirements-conflict loops are already present at the bounded history window's **12:00 UTC / 07:00 CDT** start. Their backend connections were opened October 5; this does **not** date the initial looping requests to October 5.
- Correction-eligibility loops first appear at **12:35:14**, **12:35:16**, **12:42:14**, **12:54:12**, **14:37:52**, **14:41:05**, and **14:59:53.707 UTC**. The first seven are on the seven distinct correction processes that are still active in the inspected snapshot. Earlier browser/private-review success can coexist with spare pool capacity; no new sign-in/frontend deployment is required to accumulate these stuck requests.
- The tenth observed loop begins **14:59:53.707 UTC / 09:59:53 CDT**; the previously established first pool-acquisition timeout is **15:00:03.631590 UTC / 10:00:03 CDT**, about ten seconds afterward. Combined with the exact persistent worker/log matches, this strongly supports pool exhaustion by internal retries as the principal remaining cause.
- Across **12:00–19:02 UTC**, the retained window contains **2,154,047 correction-eligibility errors** on seven processes plus **347,450 requirement-revision errors** on three. There are no other 40001 message classes in this captured window.
- Existing traffic after the lock repair: password-token POST **200** at **18:59:28.796** and **19:01:34.623 UTC**, followed by Auth user GET **200** at **18:59:30.482** and **19:01:36.093 UTC**. Subsequent work GETs and companion RPC POSTs return **503/PGRST002**, within about 1.5–2.3 seconds. This is a post-password Data API failure, not evidence of incorrect credentials. These were already-existing requests, not agent-generated attempts; operator identity and exact preview path are not established from origin-only referrers.
- Post-repair API logs show **45 PGRST003** and **24 PGRST002** messages in the same bounded 18:56:49–19:02 window. Schema-cache events follow DDL, but its reload is occurring while the ten internal retry workers remain occupied. PGRST002 means schema-cache database connection failure; a reload failure in this condition does not by itself establish malformed migration SQL.

**Source trace.** Correction basis/state entered the existing Phase 6 source in **b285f67** (2026-10-11 18:27:54 CDT), in `20261011200000_phase_6_correction_admin_drafts.sql`; later revisions retained the business-conflict 40001 codes. It was already part of the first working October 13 preview rather than an intervening deploy between working and failing login. The client `dashboard/correction-draft.js` calls correction-state for FIELD_COMPLETE work and handles rejection; the provider's internal retry prevents that rejection from returning normally. Preserve these parity checks when preparing the next migration.

**Official provider confirmation.** [Supabase's current troubleshooting guidance](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/troubleshooting/high-cpu-and-infinite-transaction-retries-when-using-custom-error-codes-in-rpc-functions-77326b) documents unbounded retries for custom 40001 errors in PostgREST 14, fixed in 16. It recommends a non-retryable exception such as **PT409** for business conflicts and clearing the already-looping processes, since fixing a function alone does not stop in-flight loops. [PostgREST 14 custom errors](https://docs.postgrest.org/en/v14/references/errors.html) defines PT409 as an HTTP conflict response, PGRST003 as pool acquisition timeout and PGRST002 as a database connection failure during schema-cache construction. We have not verified the precise reason for the separate historical fresh-token PGRST303; it must not be conflated with the proven current loop mechanism.

Privacy-safe Supabase `query_logs` returned a provider error rather than request-level evidence, so avoid attributing a specific response to the user's attempt.

Once dispatched, company identity cannot change across clients"**, and §3 says existing unknown-company WOs remain unconfigured/unsendable until deliberately bound. A previously-unknown company is *not* an old cross-client binding, but the installed guard prohibits *all* post-evidence initial bindings, including verified legacy TEST cases. The separate Phase 6 correction basis correctly demands current completed **client** work, so this exact completed TEST job cannot progress to an eligible same-WO correction under the currently installed RPC. **STOP the affected late-binding path** rather than service-role SQL update, bypass/disable guard, misrepresent UI control visibility as draft eligibility, mutate historical photo/Finish association, or make the operator repeat already-passed photo tests.

## Phase 6B/6C — protected old run blocked new phone assignment; isolated cache fix verified (2026-10-14)

**Physical failure, not a failed dispatch.** The operator shared the Samsung internal Android `TEST-0003-DASHBOARD` card showing the historical September 14 due date, photo requirements optional and `Status: Needs review` with **"Your saved offline progress is preserved. This assignment could not be confirmed."** The screenshot proves the old prior-run card remained visible after the controlled follow-up dispatch. It does not by itself prove whether another card was also rendered farther down the list. Stop the Run 2 field test: do not Accept/Start/Finish or take photos on the old conflicted card; do not clear app data, reinstall or resend Dispatch.

**Independent live server parity.** Team TEST `vyocaujuwrivoqynvitm` still has one permanent `TEST-0003-DASHBOARD` WO, current Run **2** `ASSIGNED`, due **2026-10-16** with new-run Total photo minimum **1**, fresh active assignment receipt **confirmed** and human Accept **not yet confirmed**. Run 2 has zero server photos/actions. Original Run 1 is FIELD_COMPLETE with one photo, two field actions, historical assignment records; unchanged client-company binding, one dispatched correction draft and zero client packages/outbox. A server receipt indicates the receipt RPC accepted the client assertion; it **does not independently prove** that the new Run 2 was durably saved to Room.

**Verified source cause.** In `CachedWorkOrderDao.replaceForOwner`, the prior run with stored evidence is rightly protected when the downloaded current run changes. However, `protectedWos` was keyed by **work-order UUID**, not run identity, and `if (!protectedWos.contains(row.workOrderId))` then suppressed insertion of a newly downloaded **different** Run 2 for that same WO. `AssignmentRepository.refresh` could still call the server assignment-received RPC after the enclosing Room transaction returned without inserting the new run. This explains the historical cached card and confirmed server receipt; it does not authorize treating Run 2 as accepted, repairing server data, deleting old rows, or bypassing local conflict protection.

**Strictly scoped corrective implementation.** On only authoritative PR #40 branch, source commit `57ce26f07a9ba72bcb04624f4c9cff94d7a45e6f` changes the skip/protection set from same-WO to exact **(WO UUID, run UUID)**. An old evidenced run is still kept with its protected conflict history, while an independently authorized fresh run can be inserted using its own assignment instance. New Robolectric regression `completedRunWithProtectedPhotoDoesNotBlockFreshFollowUpAssignment` (commit `57f13594ab7208e5bee98cc1de68a44c204fc687`) tests the old original photo persists while Run 2 is present, actionable for separate `ACK`, has one-photo total, stays intact across refresh/restart and retains separate photo/run identity. No Room schema, field-action protocol, photo owner, Supabase endpoint, dashboard/runtime layout or FPP files changed.

**Internal test-build identity and exact automated evidence.** Candidate/recovery version pair was advanced to **24/25** in `app/build.gradle` to permit safe in-place future update (commit `f8c43bfec3b211fa26b4c0edb77043b219ed77f0`), with matching **24/25** pin in the existing artifact identity script (commit `bb07467fd91a6e763991c61fa0d2bab8f7804a44`). One duplicate intermediate dashboard browser job timed out, while its counterpart succeeded. Do not treat those superseded intermediate checks as final PASS. **Exact final runtime `bb07467fd91a6e763991c61fa0d2bab8f7804a44` has 7/7 GitHub CI checks SUCCESS**: 2 Android (focused and full JVM, unchanged Room-5 schema exports, signed candidate and recovery builds), 2 database, 2 dashboard, 1 governance. ## Phase 5D — OAuth token POST transport failed; pre-consent endpoint probe added (2026-10-14)

**Real operator evidence and precise boundary.** The operator ran the existing local `ci/authorize_phase5_workspace_provider.mjs` from PR #40 on Linux Mint, signed in to Google's OAuth authorization in the approved Work Chrome profile and reported the terminal's exact resulting fixed code **`BLOCKED: TOKEN_EXCHANGE_UNAVAILABLE`**. The browser had returned an OAuth code to the active local callback. In the prior source, this code is thrown **only by the catch around the network `fetcher('https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token', POST)` for the authorization-code exchange**, before parsing a token response, before Drive `about.get`, before Drive `files.get` and before protected credential persistence. Thus this checkpoint does **not** establish whether Google accepted credentials or whether the root Drive ID is right; it establishes only that the laptop's Node HTTPS token exchange threw a transport exception. Do not infer a wrong Google account, invalid shared-drive access, failed OAuth consent, blocked scope, or a Google Cloud project/billing issue from this fixed code. No credential receipt, Drive Send, package release or worker activation was recorded. Avoid reusing the one-time Google authorization code.

**Safe fix / diagnostic at original owner.** The source-only TEST bootstrap on the same existing draft PR was amended, **not** the main trusted backend refresher, Drive adapter, team runtime or dashboard. Add `checkTokenEndpoint` using a fixed `https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token` **POST** with the dummy body `grant_type=fwh_connectivity_probe` (no client ID, secret, token, private folder or user account). The existing helper runs this no-credentials transport preflight **before displaying any consent URL**; any 4xx HTTP response proves the specific POST request reached Google, while an exception maps to stable nonsecret DNS/timeout/connection/TLS codes and a failed 5xx/2xx is fail-closed. The real authorization code exchange now shares the fixed nonsecret transport-code classifier, never exception text or request body. Added focused synthetic tests for successful dummy HTTP 400, no-token/no-secret transport, denied responses and safe fixed-code classification. Git commits `69321897e0f6be3b533ad27aa9ee2718551b274e` and `80f7f5c7d78e143a91a772697b2ad2353e66e1d0`. The previous distinct error-stage fix on `44c806545a8cb9b9e2129b231de7dfa3e12b64fe` passed 7/7 GitHub checks.

**Next smallest physical provider question (no OAuth consent repeat).** Ask the operator to run a single credential-free comparison on their own laptop between `node fetch` and `curl` to the same **fixed** Google token endpoint using the dummy probe. An HTTP 400 means the network request was delivered; a Node-only failure can isolate local Node networking/proxy/TLS/DNS differences; simultaneous failures indicate a broader endpoint/network problem. No operator OAuth password, code, access token, refresh token or credential JSON need be submitted. Treat results as a new read-only physical environment observation. Don't rerun OAuth until this boundary is reconciled, then resume the already-approved real provider identity/root checks in dependency order.

## Phase 5D — Linux Mint Node 20 IPv4-only OAuth bootstrap repair (2026-10-14)

**Actual user-controlled provider evidence / BLOCKED.** User ran a **credential-free** POST with `curl` to Google's fixed token endpoint and received expected **HTTP 400**, whereas Node v`20.20.2` default `fetch` on the same laptop reported **`ETIMEDOUT`**. The one-off probe using `node --dns-result-order=ipv4first` returned **HTTP 400**, indicating IPv4 preference could reach the endpoint at least once; neither result proves that all future Node connections succeed. The subsequent actual TEST bootstrap checkout used `NODE_OPTIONS=--dns-result-order=ipv4first` with the previously approved TEST-only configuration and stopped **before displaying a Google consent link** with **`BLOCKED: TOKEN_ENDPOINT_TIMEOUT`**. The real authorization-code token POST has a bounded 20-second transport deadline, no blind retry with a consumed code. ## Phase 5D — exact client Shared Drive and remote filename receipt binding (2026-10-15)

**Governed scope.** Approved Level-3 Phase 5/6 on existing draft PR #40, branch feat/phase-5-6-dashboard-workflow, original rollback base 62244e2c4c11831d4e8104821f851f19124b2616. Existing backend file-transfer owner only; changes to upload-one.mjs, worker-runtime.mjs, their tests and this authoritative record. No client Send, permission, cleanup, Auth, Android, FPP, new branch, new queue, new credential, migration or remote write.

**Concrete issue and fix.** The worker validated the immutable package folder in its expected client Shared Drive before sending bytes, but its final independent JPEG/manifest verifyFile call checked size, SHA-256, MIME, parent, and appProperties WITHOUT requiring the exact frozen file name or expected Shared Drive on the file itself. A post-upload rename or cross-Drive move could therefore have been falsely accepted as a final receipt. The existing transferOnePreparedChunk now requires a trusted syntactically valid expectedDriveId before any provider/session operation, and sends BOTH expectedDriveId and plan.name into the existing Drive verifyFile adapter on both completion paths. The existing continueTrustedDeliveryFile forwards the already checked expectedDriveId. No new provider client or write code.

## Phase 5D — durable one-shot upload-session initiation fence (2026-10-15)

**Scope/line:** approved Level 3 Phase 5D on existing DRAFT PR #40, `feat/phase-5-6-dashboard-workflow`, scope key `dashboard-workflow-enhancement-plan`. Restore source to previous tested documentation head `aaa59b654957a5a7afd8e58f77f28cbf2bd13a54` to roll back these candidate-only additions; original PR integration rollback is `62244e2c4c11831d4e8104821f851f19124b2616`. This is a **source-only** additional guard; no live migration, Edge deployment, worker activation, package Send, provider write, secrets, phone install, deletion or FPP action.

**Defect/reason:** The candidate trusted Google upload worker persisted a successfully returned encrypted resumable session before uploading any bytes but did not persist an **attempt marker before the initial Google session-start POST**. On a lost POST response or unsuccessful session-journal write, later `service_read_upload_session` reported no session, enabling another initiation for the same reserved file identity within an otherwise-current worker lease. A remote ID reservation alone is not proof of an already attempted session start. This is the same uncertainty class exposed by the operator's original disposable TEST start; the original TEST journal and Google file ID remain untouched and blocked.

**Source correction:** Add additive migration `20261015213000_phase_5_upload_session_start_fence.sql` with private RLS-protected, append-only one-shot `client_delivery_upload_starts` keyed by original file plan, plus service-only `service_begin_upload_session`. The existing current-lease/plan check and transactional unique insertion durably mark `START_ATTEMPTED` **before** the trusted worker can issue Google's start POST. The existing session-read RPC now denies a missing session if that immutable start marker exists. A recovered encrypted session still resumes by its exact saved URL, and older valid existing ciphertext remains readable; absent marker after a failed RPC never grants the worker a POST because the begin RPC itself must confirm success before provider access. Unknown POST, lost session URL, stale/missing lease and duplicate invocation all fail closed. No new remote ID, retry authority, private photo access, cleanup or production activation is created. Existing phone photos and frozen provider identity remain unchanged.

**Coverage and exact automated outcome:** `client-delivery-upload.test.mjs` proves mark-before-POST, no prepared bytes before stored session, refusal after lost start response or lost session journal, and missing marker RPC refusal. `client-delivery-joined.test.mjs` preserves the existing independent exact client Shared Drive/filename/receipt/Storage-range scenarios with a correctly modeled durable fake marker. `client-delivery-ledger.test.mjs` verifies fenced backend RPC arguments. New `phase_5_upload_start_gate.sql` is invoked in existing database workflow and checks enabled RLS, no direct marker insert privileges, service-only RPC execution and rejection of an unclaimed worker lease. The initial runtime `24fa60eb8466e1e4ee8f68d63d62a576f87f9467` passed Database but FAILED Admin Dashboard `38810101248` with four old joined-fixture failures (`UPLOAD_SESSION_START_FENCE_REQUIRED`); do not count it as green. The correction modified **only that test fixture**, not the new fence. Independently, destination-list reads were only gated by a request counter, so an old-company verified folder list needed explicit per-current-account-and-WO invalidation when closing the job or signing out. The defects affected dashboard preference safety / client company name isolation, **not** remote Google object writes.

**Exact source change:** Kept the existing verified-destination controls and the new inactive/unverified proposal surface, all in the same `dashboard/folder-layout.js` owner. A preference save now captures the existing selected destination/company, expected revision, layout and one unique action UUID; HTTP 408/429/5xx, failed transport and malformed success acknowledgements retain frozen inputs and expose **Retry exact preference save** using the same UUID/CAS revision. Neither account, destination nor folder preference is editable under an uncertain write. Definitive 4xx rejection clears only local pending state and requires refetch before a new choice; success requires matching destination, company, selected layout and a valid returned revision. No success is fabricated after timeout. Verified destination list reads now validate complete typed UUID/layout/revision and duplicate-free IDs and reject responses unless auth token, caller UUID, current WO and request generation still match. `ClientFolderLayout.reset()` empties verified destinations, invalidates pending list reads / preference writes, and also resets the already-supported inactive proposal. Existing `dashboard/client-release.js` invokes that reset through the established job switch / close owner. No new backend authority, secrets, Google call, alternate auth or persistent browser account model was added.

**Tests and corrections.** Existing `tests/dashboard/browser/workspace.cjs` uses only synthetic server responses: a verified client choice, a forced HTTP 503 preference save, frozen selector / refresh, retry of **identical action UUID**, exact success and disabled duplicate-save; a second fixture starts a deliberately delayed destination-list request in an open job, closes the job before the response, verifies cleared options, releases the old reply and asserts prior client names never reappear or persist through signout. Test staging only required a correction: first candidate `e8cba83289ad20cf8ce8fa11582408c6be8fd1d3` FAILED Admin browser CI **`38837974572`** by attempting to click the Delivery tab *after the modal had been closed*; it was a fixture sequence issue rather than an app source failure. Corrected the fixture to reopen a real job before staging the slow call and close normally via existing Cancel. Final tested runtime head **`36ec5a6a39cf7733a997b44f63c12905cf9fbe5b`**: Admin Dashboard Gate CI **`38838218216` SUCCESS** (real disposable Chromium), FWH Database Gate CI **`38838218182` SUCCESS**, Android Team Client CI **`38838218184` SUCCESS** (JVM/build/candidate evidence). The complete CI was run on one exact corrected runtime head. Modified runtime files are limited to `dashboard/folder-layout.js`, `dashboard/client-release.js`, and `tests/dashboard/browser/workspace.cjs`. Browser mocks/CI do not prove a real Google destination.

**External status and next real gate:** No change to Supabase migrations, Auth, Storage, hosted Edge/dashboard, Google Workspace or original disposable provider journal/ID, live clients, signing material, Android installed state, or Field Photo Prep. Last verified live Team state is 99/101 migrations with unverified proposal and one-shot Google upload marker unapplied; no verified/active client destination, worker-ready record or queued delivery. Original operator TEST Google file remains `START_ATTEMPTED`/original-ID `PROVIDER_IDENTITY_UNCERTAIN` without resumable URL or first PUT; **never** begin again, allocate a replacement, reset/rotate the journal or request operator Bash. Continue only approved independent source work; real original-ID reconciliation, exact private Storage 206 → Google receipt, email sender/link, Web Push/OS, combined phone/provider proof, deliberate live parity and **explicit separate Level-3 premerge operator approval** remain unresolved. PR #40 remains draft/unmerged and whole Phases 5/6 remain in progress.


## Phase 5A/5D — preserved client-folder retries across Auth token rotation (2026-10-15)

**Authority and scope.** Continued the same approved complete parent Phase 5/6 plan on `feat/phase-5-6-dashboard-workflow`, sole DRAFT PR #40, scope `dashboard-workflow-enhancement-plan`. Level 2 existing dashboard session/client folder preference correction inside the governing Level 3 implementation line; no alternate provider or user interface, no source migration, no authorization expansion. Required governance, current roadmap, complete shared-access amendment and the current active implementation record were consulted; explicit separate Level 3 premerge approval is not granted. Narrow rollback is `780b897387a3416dd795631bf67782d6deb348f9`; broader integration rollback remains `62244e2c4c11831d4e8104821f851f19124b2616`.

**Real defect and minimal implementation.** `dashboard/folder-layout.js` compared an asynchronous verified destination read / exact CAS preference save / unverified destination proposal's *captured bearer access-token string* to the current access-token value. The existing trusted `dashboard/session.js` owner automatically rotates the bearer for the **same account** without changing the session identity or user. Therefore a genuine authorized response arriving after a token refresh could be discarded without restoring pending/busy buttons, falsely stranding an exact-action retry. Revised only the pre-existing client-folder settings module to compare (i) the `adminSessionEpoch` generation owned by the existing session module, (ii) the **same immutable account UUID**, (iii) the same selected WO and (iv) the module's own exact request/proposal revision, while retaining a nonempty active token. Refresh within the same session no longer invalidates exact-action results; any signout/relogin increments the auth epoch and disallows old results, even for a later login of the same user. No credentials or signed URLs are stored by this correction. All proposal/backend writes still go through existing `adminFetch`, which refreshes and rebinds the bearer using its authoritative session owner; the SQL role/org/revision authorizations, client destination verification and explicit Send gate are untouched.

**Test and precise evidence.** Extended the existing disposable Chrome workspace test `tests/dashboard/browser/workspace.cjs`: after a forced synthetic HTTP 503 ambiguous layout save, rotate `accessToken` to a synthetic same-user bearer **without** incrementing the existing auth session epoch, then retry the **same frozen** action UUID and verify confirmed save, choice and no duplicate send. The existing inactive/unverified proposal response and withheld Send, late old-job destination reply and signout clearing checks remain exercised. A mocked bearer rotation is **not** a real production Supabase Auth reauthorization proof; account-switch isolation remains tested separately in the existing browser suite. Exact final runtime commit **`5cca19ea8c4aafac08f6de1928a7b143fe4f3821`** completed **Admin Dashboard Gate CI `38839425353` SUCCESS**, **Database Gate CI `38839425287` SUCCESS**, and **Android Team Client CI `38839425329` SUCCESS**, including existing real disposable browser/PostgreSQL/JVM tests. Only `dashboard/folder-layout.js` and the existing browser regression were modified; no unrelated workflow, direct client photo access, protected original, file identity, worker/retry/cleanup predicate, permissions or server implementation was changed.

**Real backend and outstanding boundaries.** Read-only Team Supabase migration inventory on 2026-10-15 remains **99 applied**, with exactly 2 source migrations intentionally *unapplied*: `20261014230000_phase_5_unverified_client_destination_proposal.sql` and `20261015213000_phase_5_upload_session_start_fence.sql`. Live delivery destinations and outbox remain empty per same-session read-only SQL. The hosted `client-delivery` Edge function remains protected TEST-only, not a live write worker. No Auth sender/redirect, verified client root, Drive credentials, original provider journal, cloud deployment, Android install, FPP app or customer data were changed. Source-only recovery email must not be claimed viable without verified sender, Auth config, fixed callback, no-signup, account/origin throttling, protected token exchange and a real permitted mailbox test; Auth's email token duration also affects other email flows, so do not change it blindly. Optional Web Push still requires actual VAPID/provider/browser/OS proof.

**Genuine stop on dependent paths.** The original disposable Google file remains `START_ATTEMPTED` with immutable original ID and no durable resumable URL or confirmed first-byte PUT. The earlier exact-ID provider check was `ORIGINAL_FILE_NOT_VERIFIED (PROVIDER_IDENTITY_UNCERTAIN)`; GET 404/410 is not proof that the first create did not reach Drive. Never blind start/resume, create an alternate ID, discard original journal, alter Workspace credentials or request operator Bash as a shortcut. Keep provider delivery/cleanup blocked until an authoritative original-ID reconcile; combine physical phone/provider/sender/push checks per approved plan and seek separate explicit Level-3 operator approval before PR merge. PR remains DRAFT/unmerged and whole parent phases remain in progress.
